## 1. Comprehensive Review of Quiz Applets

Across the repository, multiple practice and assessment engines are implemented to reinforce lexical acquisition, phonological decoding, and grammatical accuracy for Greek 6th-grade pupils (CEFR A2/A2+).

### V1 Quiz Engine (`app.js`)

Implemented in `unit<N>/app.js` and rendered via `unit<N>/index.html`. It contains two real-time randomized multiple-choice applets:

- **A. Greek Listening Challenge (`#viewQuiz`)**:
  - **Prompt**: Spoken English word audio (`padId_word.mp3`) synthesized via Sonia Neural AI (British prosody) or Google TTS.
  - **Question Task**: Pupils listen to the spoken word and must identify the correct Greek translation (`meaning_gr`).
  - **Options Layout**: 4 buttons labelled A, B, C, D displaying **Greek translation text only** (no English text or giveaway emojis).
  - **Distractor Architecture**: For target item $T$, 3 distractors $D_1, D_2, D_3$ are sampled uniformly at random from the active unit's vocabulary list ($D_i.id \neq T.id$).
  - **Feedback Loop**: When clicked, correct cards highlight green, wrong cards highlight red. The correct card dynamically reveals the English word (`🇬🇧 word`) and IPA transcription for immediate pedagogical reinforcement.
  - **Streak & Scoring**: Tracks total attempts, correct score, and streak (`🔥 5`).

- **B. English Definition Challenge (`#viewDefQuiz`)**:
  - **Prompt**: Spoken English word audio (`padId_word.mp3`).
  - **Question Task**: Pupils listen to the word and select the matching CEFR A2 English definition (`definition_en`).
  - **Options Layout**: 4 buttons displaying English definition sentences.
  - **Distractor Architecture**: 3 random definitions drawn from other items in the unit.
  - **Feedback Loop**: Reveals target word, part of speech, and Greek translation on the correct card.

### V2 Challenge Arena (`app_v2.js`)

Implemented in `unit<N>/app_v2.js` and rendered via `unit<N>/v2.html`. It integrates a gamified 4-mode testing suite (`#panel-challenges`):

| Mode ID | Name | Prompt Type | Question / Target | Option Format & Distractors |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `greek` | Greek Meaning Matcher | Neural Audio (Word) | Match spoken word to Greek | 4 Greek translations (1 Target + 3 Unit Distractors) |
| `def` | Definition Matcher | Neural Audio (Word) | Match spoken word to English definition | 4 English definitions (1 Target + 3 Unit Distractors) |
| `gapfill` | Sentence Gap-Fill | Neural Audio (Example) | Listen to sentence; identify missing word | 4 English words with POS tags (1 Target + 3 Unit Distractors) |
| `fact` | Fact-Buster True/False | Text Statement | Verify factual/geographical claim from texts | 2 Options: TRUE vs. FALSE with explanatory rationale |


### V2 Collocation Matcher Applet

Rendered in `unit<N>/v2.html` (`#panel-collocations`). Pupils pair high-frequency verbs (`share`, `grow`, `drop`, `make`) with curricular complements (`borders with`, `citrus fruit & tea`, `below zero`). All active unselected phrases in the right-hand column serve as semantic and syntactic distractors during the pairing interaction.

### Workbook Interactive Quiz Applets

Configured in `unit<N>/data/unit<N>_workbook_data.json` and rendered in the `#panel-workbook` interactive tab:

- **Grammar Tense Chooser**: Binary or ternary multiple choice (e.g. `do you do` vs. `are you doing`; `much` vs. `a lot of`; `if Terry comes` reactions).
- **Odd-One-Out Lexical Challenges**: 4-word clusters where 3 share a lexical field and 1 acts as the odd distractor (e.g., musical instruments, transport modes, parts of speech).
- **Matching Definitions**: Matching target vocabulary words with definitions where adjacent definitions serve as active distractors.


### Distractor Generation & Pedagogical Analysis

1. **Dynamic In-Unit Pooling**: In the current V1 and V2 engines, distractors are chosen at runtime via `Math.random()` over the unit's vocabulary list. This guarantees infinite replayability without hardcoded repetition.
2. **Homogeneous Part-of-Speech Matching (Pedagogical Observation)**: In the standard random selection, a verb target might occasionally be paired with noun distractors. In the Gap-Fill challenge (`gapfill`), pairing with same-part-of-speech distractors (e.g. all verbs or all adjectives) provides higher pedagogical diagnostic value.
3. **Static Workbook Distractors**: The workbook multiple-choice items feature curriculum-authored distractors targeting common Greek L1 interference errors (e.g., confusing *much* with *a lot of* for affirmative countable/uncountable nouns).

## 3. Workbook Multiple-Choice Activities & Static Distractors

This section catalogues the explicit closed multiple-choice items, selection quizzes, and odd-one-out exercises defined in `unit<N>_workbook_data.json` across all units.

### UNIT1 Workbook Multiple-Choice & Selection Exercises

#### B. Grammar — 3. Choose the Correct Verb Form (Petros & George) (Type: `closed`)

| Item # | Context / Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Explicit Distractors |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | What | **`are you doing`** | `do you do` |
| 2 | Well, I | **`'m listening`** | `listen` |
| 3 | and I | **`'m playing`** | `play` |
| 4 | I always | **`listen`** | `am always listening` |
| 5 | when I | **`play`** | `am playing` |
| 6 | What kind of music do you usually | **`listen`** | `are you usually listening` |
| 7 | I usually | **`listen`** | `'m usually listening` |
| 8 | to classical music but today I | **`'m listening`** | `listen` |


### UNIT2 Workbook Multiple-Choice & Selection Exercises

#### B. Grammar — 4. Choose the correct answer (Type: `closed`)

| Item # | Context / Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Explicit Distractors |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | There is _____ bread on the table. | **`a loaf of`** | `a`, `an` |
| 2 | We haven’t got _____ butter in the fridge. | **`any`** | `a`, `some` |
| 3 | _____ orange juice have we got? | **`how much`** | `how`, `how many` |
| 4 | We need _____ egg for this cake. | **`an`** | `much`, `many` |
| 5 | Mary has got _____ money in her pocket. | **`some`** | `a`, `an` |
| 6 | Terry wants to buy _____ beer for his father. | **`two bottles of`** | `many`, `a packet of` |
| 7 | I haven’t got _____ money today. | **`much`** | `some`, `many` |
| 8 | Susan drinks _____ orange juice. | **`a lot of`** | `much`, `many` |
| 9 | There is _____ sugar in the pot. Let’s buy some. | **`little`** | `few`, `any` |
| 10 | There aren’t _____ eggs left. How are we going to make the omelette? | **`many`** | `much`, `few` |


#### B. Grammar — 7. Paul’s shopping mistakes (Type: `closed`)

| Item # | Context / Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Explicit Distractors |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Example: I want to spend much time looking around. | **`a lot of`** | `much` |
| 2 | I don’t eat many frozen food. | **`much`** | `many` |
| 3 | I usually buy many different vegetables. | **`a lot of`** | `many` |
| 4 | I prefer much apples. | **`many`** | `much` |
| 5 | I don’t eat many meat. | **`much`** | `many` |
| 6 | I always buy any fish. | **`some`** | `any` |


### UNIT3 Workbook Multiple-Choice & Selection Exercises

| Item # | Context / Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Explicit Distractors |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Can we spend the night with you here? (1) We feel so tired! | **`c. We are travelling as far as New York but it’s already dark.`** | `a. We could never accept money for a service as small as this.`, `b. You look so exhausted!`, `d. They are freshly baked.` |
| 2 | We are about to turn in, but please, welcome! (2) Our house is not as luxurious as a hotel but it’s cosy! | **`b. You look so exhausted!`** | `a. We could never accept money for a service as small as this.`, `c. We are travelling as far as New York but it’s already dark.`, `d. They are freshly baked.` |
| 3 | Please, have a cup of hot tea and some cakes. (3) — Thank you. I haven’t tasted cakes as delicious as these before! | **`d. They are freshly baked.`** | `a. We could never accept money for a service as small as this.`, `b. You look so exhausted!`, `c. We are travelling as far as New York but it’s already dark.` |
| 4 | Please allow me to pay for the room and food. — Oh, no! (4). We consider you as our guests. | **`a. We could never accept money for a service as small as this.`** | `b. You look so exhausted!`, `c. We are travelling as far as New York but it’s already dark.`, `d. They are freshly baked.` |

#### 8. Multiple Choice Tense Quiz (Type: `multiple_choice`)

| Question # | Question Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Explicit Distractors |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 1. Have you ever ridden a horse? | **`c. No, I haven't.`** | `a. last year.`, `b. Not at all.` |
| 2 | 2. Have you ever swum in a river? | **`c. Yes, I have. I swam last summer.`** | `a. Yes, I did.`, `b. Not until yesterday.` |
| 3 | 3. Has Tom been studying English for many years? | **`a. Yes, for 5 years.`** | `b. No, he didn't.`, `c. Yes, he does.` |
| 4 | 4. When did you start piano lessons? | **`b. last week.`** | `a. since 2006.`, `c. next year.` |
| 5 | 5. Have you broken a class record? | **`c. No, I haven't. I think I must try harder.`** | `a. Not until last year.`, `b. Yes, I did. I ate 3 ice-creams in four minutes.` |


### UNIT8 Workbook Multiple-Choice & Selection Exercises

#### 6. Conversational Reactions (Type: `multiple_choice`)

| Item # | Context / Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Explicit Distractors |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | If Terry comes, he will play the violin! | **`a. Good idea!`** | `b. Yes, I can!`, `c. Sure. Let's try!` |
| 2 | If we go to Paris, we can visit the Eiffel Tower. | **`b. That's right.`** | `a. Don't mention it.`, `c. That's wrong.` |
| 3 | What will happen if its mother doesn't feed it? | **`b. We will!`** | `a. Sorry about it.`, `c. What's that?` |
| 4 | If I finish my homework at 8, I may watch television for some time. | **`c. Good! Try to finish early.`** | `a. Don't make it!`, `b. That's enough!` |
| 5 | If I won a lot of money, I would spend it all in one day! | **`a. That's not sensible!`** | `b. Sure, let's go.`, `c. What about it?` |


### UNIT9 Workbook Multiple-Choice & Selection Exercises

#### A. Vocabulary — 3. Odd Word Out (Type: `odd_one_out`)

| Set # | Candidate Word Options | Odd Word (Target) | Common Trait / Pedagogical Rationale |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| a | `sea turtle`, `male`, `seal`, `bear` | **`male`** | *'male' is a gender descriptor, while all others are wild animal species.* |
| b | `industrial waste`, `litter`, `rubbish`, `species` | **`species`** | *'species' is a biological grouping, while all others are forms of trash and pollution.* |
| c | `tide`, `wave`, `starfish`, `sea` | **`starfish`** | *'starfish' is a living marine organism, while all others are physical ocean features.* |
| d | `pollute`, `save`, `protect`, `clean` | **`pollute`** | *'pollute' means causing ecological damage, while the other three verbs mean protecting the environment.* |


#### B. Grammar — 6. Choose Because vs. Because of (Type: `multiple_choice`)

| Item # | Prompt Context | Target Answer (Key) | Contrast / Distractor |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | The beach was dirty ______ careless visitors left plastic litter. | **`because`** | `because of` *(followed by a full clause with subject and verb)* |
| 2 | Many marine animals suffer ______ water pollution. | **`because of`** | `because` *(followed by a noun phrase)* |
| 3 | Plants grow slowly ______ acid rain. | **`because of`** | `because` *(followed by a noun phrase)* |
| 4 | People celebrated Earth Day ______ they wanted to protect the planet. | **`because`** | `because of` *(followed by a full clause)* |


### UNIT10 Workbook Multiple-Choice & Selection Exercises

#### A. Vocabulary — 4. Match the Following with Their Opposites (Type: `matching_pairs`)

| Target Adjective | Correct Antonym (Key) | Explicit Distractors | Category / Pedagogical Rationale |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **handsome** | **`ugly`** | `straight`, `kind`, `tight`, `tidy`, `good`, `boring` | *Facial & physical appearance* |
| **evil** | **`good`** | `ugly`, `straight`, `kind`, `tight`, `tidy`, `boring` | *Moral character traits* |
| **baggy** | **`tight`** | `ugly`, `straight`, `kind`, `tidy`, `good`, `boring` | *Clothing fit & silhouette* |
| **nasty** | **`kind`** | `ugly`, `straight`, `tight`, `tidy`, `good`, `boring` | *Interpersonal behavior* |
| **scruffy** | **`tidy`** | `ugly`, `straight`, `kind`, `tight`, `good`, `boring` | *Grooming & clothes presentation* |
| **interesting** | **`boring`** | `ugly`, `straight`, `kind`, `tight`, `tidy`, `good` | *Film / story appraisal* |
| **crooked** | **`straight`** | `ugly`, `kind`, `tight`, `tidy`, `good`, `boring` | *Physical alignment (teeth / line)* |


#### A. Vocabulary — 9. Choose the Correct Participle (-ed or -ing) (Type: `multiple_choice`)

| Item # | Sentence Prompt | Target Answer (Key) | Distractor Form | Pedagogical Rule |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Their performance was awful. They were all... | **`disappointed`** | `disappointing` | *-ed expresses the emotional letdown felt by the actors* |
| 2 | Your ideas for the costumes of the hero sound... | **`exciting`** | `excited` | *-ing describes the exciting quality of the costume ideas* |
| 3 | I was really ______ to hear the end of the story. | **`surprised`** | `surprising` | *-ed expresses the listener's feeling of astonishment* |
| 4 | I was scared to death. The film was so... | **`frightening`** | `frightened` | *-ing describes the scary characteristic of the film* |
| 5 | I have nothing to do. I feel so... | **`bored`** | `boring` | *-ed expresses the speaker's state of boredom* |


#### B. Grammar — 4. Where Can You See the Signs? (Type: `matching_signs`)

| Public Passive Sign | Location (Key) | Explicit Distractors | Context & Grammar Focus |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **SMOKING IS FORBIDDEN** | **`on a train`** | `in a hotel restaurant`, `on a piece of clothing`, `in a shop`, `at a concert hall`, `on a beach` | *Present Simple Passive prohibition* |
| **DINNER IS SERVED AT 7.00** | **`in a hotel restaurant`** | `on a train`, `on a piece of clothing`, `in a shop`, `at a concert hall`, `on a beach` | *Passive scheduling notice* |
| **CREDIT CARDS ARE ACCEPTED** | **`in a shop`** | `on a train`, `in a hotel restaurant`, `on a piece of clothing`, `at a concert hall`, `on a beach` | *Passive transaction policy* |
| **IT'S MADE OF PURE COTTON** | **`on a piece of clothing`** | `on a train`, `in a hotel restaurant`, `in a shop`, `at a concert hall`, `on a beach` | *Passive material description* |
| **YOU ARE REQUESTED TO HAVE YOUR MOBILES SWITCHED OFF** | **`at a concert hall`** | `on a train`, `in a hotel restaurant`, `in a shop`, `on a piece of clothing`, `on a beach` | *Formal passive request* |
| **PETS ARE NOT ALLOWED** | **`on a beach`** | `on a train`, `in a hotel restaurant`, `in a shop`, `on a piece of clothing`, `at a concert hall` | *Passive environmental rule* |
